## Temples Plague

The Temples Plague was a disease that struck Calimshan and Tashalar in the .

Acquisition
The disease was spread via touch with an infected individual, even if they were unaware that they were infected.

Effects
It caused fever and accumulation of fluid in the lungs.

History
The disease was concentrated in Almraiven, Calimport, and the cities of Tashalar. It led to the deaths of about 7% of the population across these regions, although Calimport fared slightly better with a casualty rate of 5%.

The disease was unknowingly spread by priests, which was how it gained its moniker of the "Temples Plague".

## Tetsu-bishi

Tetsu-bishi were weapons unique to the continent of Kara-Tur.

Description
Tetsu-bishi were small caltrops used by ninjas. Each tetsu-bishi was designed as a spiked ball or four-sided diamond about  wide, ensuring that, no matter how it landed, at least one spike pointed upwards. These devices were strategically deployed on the ground to create a highly treacherous walking surface, effectively impeding or slowing the movement of both individuals and animals. Their design rendered them ineffective when thrown directly at opponents. Instead, tetsu-bishi were cast onto the ground near pursuers, allowing the ninja to swiftly escape.

## Temple of Tymora (Port Nyanzaru)

The Temple of Tymora in Port Nyanzaru, Chult was a place of worship dedicated to Tymora, the goddess of good fortune.

Location
The temple lay on the floor of the ravine that defined the slum district of Malar's Throat.

Structure
It was a sturdy and fortified structure of heavy stone that emphasized safety over elegance.

Services
The temple was known to reward those who protected or served the people of Malar's Throat, such as with gifts of tej or healing potions.

Whenever Malar's Throat came under attack from undead or flooded during heavy rains, the Temple of Tymora served as a safe refuge for those who were not able to flee the ravine.

History
In the century following the disappearance of Ubtao, Tymora became one of the more popular Faerûnian gods among the people of Port Nyanzaru, and the poor folk of Malar's Throat came to ascribe what little luck they had in life to her. By the late 15 century DR, foreign preachers were no longer widely welcome in the city owing to a history of exploitation, and so the Tymoran priesthood had come to be composed of native Chultans.

## Temple of Gond (Port Nyanzaru)

The Temple of Gond in Port Nyanzaru, Chult, was a place of worship dedicated to Gond, worshiped as the god of crafting.

Location
The temple lay in Port Nyanzaru's Merchants' Ward between the eastern foot of Temple Hill and the Royal Docks.

Structure
It was a boxy structure of stone. In place of a traditional forge, the temple had an immense fountain as its centerpiece. This fountain took advantage of Port Nyanzaru's naturally high water pressure passing through nozzles and valves to create jets of water that constantly shifted and formed intricate shapes.

History
In the century following the disappearance of Ubtao, Gond became one of the more popular Faerûnian gods among the people of Port Nyanzaru, notably its many artisans, craftspeople, hydro-engineers, ivory carvers, and smiths. By the late 15 century DR, foreign preachers were no longer widely welcome in the city, owing to a history of exploitation, and so the Gondar priesthood had came to be composed of native Chultans.

Appearances

## Temple of Sune (Port Nyanzaru)

The Temple of Sune in Port Nyanzaru, Chult was a place of worship dedicated to Sune, worshiped as the godess of love and beauty.

Location
The temple sat on a small, rocky island in the Bay of Chult just off the coast of Port Nyanzaru, and was considered to be part of the city's Merchants' Ward. It sat atop a cliff, and could only be accessed either by a small harbor at the base of a winding and steep staircase or via an elevated bridge between the island and the northside of Temple Hill.

Structure
The outer walls of the temple were lined with mirrors, which could make it appear that the roof was floating in the air when seen from a distance.

Services
Visitors to the temple could visit the public baths or receive instruction from the Sunite devotees about improving their appearance, such as learning how to apply make-up, style their hair, or dress fashionably and appropriately for their station and body type. Both the temple's congregation as well as casual visitors could access the temple's many services, and those who the priests saw as ugly or lacking charisma were doted on with advice and sympathy.

History
In the century following the disappearance of Ubtao, Sune became one of the more popular Faerûnian gods among the people of Port Nyanzaru driven by their appreciation of not only love and beauty, but good hygiene. The temple also sponsored and operated the city's public bathhouse in the Market Ward.

By the late 15 century DR, foreign preachers were no longer widely welcome in the city owing to a history of exploitation, and so the Sunite priesthood had come to be composed of native Chultans. Notable followers of Sune in the city at this time included the tabaxi minstrel known as Birdsong.

Appendix
Appearances

References

## Temple of Savras (Port Nyanzaru)

The Temple of Savras in Port Nyanzaru, Chult, was a place of worship dedicated to Savras, the god of diviners, fate, fortune tellers, and wizards.

Location
The temple lay in Port Nyanzaru's Merchants' Ward atop Temple Hill, and was in fact the namesake of that hill.

Structure
The temple was a grand, domed building with a tiled roof designed to resemble an unblinking eye staring up into the sky.

Services
The people of Port Nyanzaru went to the temple to ask for a peek into the future to help them make romantic, business, or other personal decisions, which the priests were happy to provide in exchange for a donation. With a gift of 25 gold pieces or more, the head priest  would agree to perform a 24-hour ritual in which they would "consult the guides" to experience a vision relating to the giver's questions. On occasion, this service might be denied to someone with evil intentions.

History
Unlike many other deities of the Faerûnian pantheon, worship of Savras in Port Nyanzaru predated the Spellplague, owing to his clerics' ability to keep tabs on the threat of the yuan-ti in the jungle.

In the century following the disappearance of Ubtao, Savras became one of the more prominent gods among the people of Port Nyanzaru, notably among those unafraid to speak the truth in the face of opposition. After the city rid itself of its Amnian colonialist rulers, foreign preachers were no longer widely welcome in the city, owing to a history of exploitation, and so the Savrasian priesthood had come to be composed of native Chultans.

By the late 15 century DR, the temple was seen as one of the oldest and most impressive buildings still standing in the city. By this time, the priesthood spent little of their time monitoring jungle monsters, and instead were sought out for much more mundane portents.

During the events of the death curse, the Red Wizards of Thay conspired to kill the temple's head priest. They sent bandits to cause a distraction while their assassin attempted to slip inside unnoticed.

Inhabitants
 Grandfather Zitembe, the head priest in the late 15 century DR.
 Inete, an acolyte in the late 15 century DR who was plagued by visions of the Red Wizards during the events of the death curse.
 Eshek, another acolyte of Savras as of the late 15 century DR.
 Father Kenda, an assistant priest to Grandfather Zitembe in the late 15 century DR.

## Tespril

Tespril was an apprentice of the Zhentarim wizard Gathlarue in the mid–14 century DR.

Description
She had large, dark eyes. Her hair was long, but after her fight with Shandril, her face became disfigured and one half of her head lost most of its hair.

Personality
She was a timid person, but not very loyal.

Activities
When in camp, she entertained the guards.

Possessions
She snatched a ring of teleportation from Gathlarue before their encounter with Shandril Shessair.

Relationships
She was younger than Mairara, her fellow apprentice under Gathlarue. Both apprentices were romantically involved with their master, and they had engaged in sadomasochism with her.

History
Tespril accompanied Gathlarue and Mairara at Marsember when they joined the search for spellfire, hunting down Shandril Shessair. She formed part of her master's retinue, as they nightly scried on their quarry. Before the attack on Irondrake Rock, their scrying had her convinced that they were going to be destroyed by Shandril's spellfire as they met in battle, and she stole a ring from her mistress when the latter became stunned as Mirt, who was traveling with Shandril at the time, destroyed a gargoyle to which Gathlarue had magically linked herself. Tespril froze during the fight with Shandril.

She survived the fight by using the ring of teleportation she'd stolen from Gathlarue, though she became badly scarred. Later, she was debriefed regarding the encounter in a meeting with Fzoul Chembryl and Sarhthor of the Zhentarim. The mage slew her at the close of the meeting, on Fzoul's ambiguous direction.

## Tecothy

Tecothy was a drow mercenary who accompanied Dhourn Ba'Tol during his search for the legendary Adamantine Forge.

History
The drow wizard Dhourn Ba'Tol vowed to share his fortune with a group of drow mercenaries in exchange for protection if they find the Adamantine Forge. The group consisted of Arcagh, Artimezt, Izwae, Moy and Tecothy.

However, Dhourn's rival Xargrim sought to find the Forge first and once met Dhourn. The rivals were both planning an ambush against each other, but began to talk of a pact, ultimately arguing until their words turned to screams. Xargrim summoned an unusual spectator adept at petrification and fled. Dhourn and his companions were all petrified for so long that Dhourn's memory shard faded.

In the, Jaheira was alerted of the mysterious return of Ketheric Thorm, now a leader of a cultist army that worshiped The Absolute. Harper Teebs was one of the agents who accompanied Jaheira into the Shadow-cursed lands surrounding the Moonrise Towers – General Thorm's seat of power.

## Teachings of Loss: The Nightsinger

Teachings of Loss: The Nightsinger was a book of Sharran dogma, printed in the ancient times, long before the 15 century DR.

Content
This Sharran book was stored in the library of the Gauntlet of Shar, an ancient temple dedicated to the Mistress of the Night and training the Dark Justiciars. The book talked about the goddess' opposition to the Nightsong as only the Nightsinger herself could silence her. It discussed the Church's belief that the creation of light was a mistake and that life itself was naught but an illusion. Because of that, the falsehoods and lies of the discordant Nightsong disturbed Shaar's oblivion. As a Dark Justiciar, those who believed these teachings took on an unholy mission to silence the song.

## Temple of the Yuir Gods

The Temple of the Yuir Gods was an ancient temple found in the Yuirwood that was transported away from the Prime Material plane to the Feywild. It was a place of worship dedicated to the Yuir pantheon.

Structure
The temple was formed out of group of trees that stood upon a hill in the Yuirwood.

Interior
The entrance hall of the temple was adorned with four, uniquely crafted, statues of the old Yuir gods: Elikarashae, Magnar the Bear, Zandilar the Dancer, and Relkath of the Infinite Branches. These statues appeared not be crafted from stone or metal, but rather grown upward from the temple's floor.

Just beyond the entranceway was the temple's ossuary. Within the heart of the temple was the thrice-twined tree.

Atmosphere
Motes of light floated throughout the temple, providing ample illumination within.

Activities
The star elves of the Yuirwood used the temple as a place of worship, but also as the holy site within which they could inter their dead.

History
Some time circa the, the cult of Voldini uncovered the ancient fey document, the Tome of Twilight Boughs, which outlined a ritual that could bring back the temple to Toril. They had the ultimate goal of returning the Yuir pantheon to prominence in the Realms.

The cultists led by Berrain Truescourge ultimately proved successful in their efforts, and completed the ritual, inadvertently corrupting nearby fey, such as pixies and dryads, in the Yuirwood.

The same night the temple returned, a group of adventurers returned the temple to the Feywild and protected the fey of the Yuirwood from utter corruption. They accomplished this feat by implanting a crystal imbued life force of a noble fey that sacrificed their life, within the temple's thrice-twined tree.

 Nimiwi, a dryad who was seperated from her tree when the temple transported to the Feywild.

## Teachings of Loss: Oblivion

Teachings of Loss: Oblivion was a Sharran text, printed before the 15 century DR.

Content
The tome was dedicated to one of the most important concepts of the Sharran faith: oblivion or emptiness, which was a holy state in the eyes of the Goddess of Loss. The holy text stated that emptiness was to be desired and pursued by Shar's devotees. They were to empty their lives of all earthly attachments and feelings to achieve purity in her eyes. The Teachings of Loss: Oblivion warned the goddess's followers of temptations of light and life as those things would only fail them in the end.

## Teachings of Loss: Light's Love

Teachings of Loss: Light's Love was a Sharran text, printed before the 15 century DR.

Content
The tome was dedicated to an important concept of the Sharran faith: the absence of love. The book preached a dogma of love being a destructive force in one's life, a type of corruption that can entrap and enthrall a person within a moment, a passing glimpse. Love's warmth and comfort were an offense to Shar's emptiness and oblivion, and as such, the Church commanded its devotees to forcefully extinguish any embers of love and affection in their hearts, leaving behind nothing by ashes and a hungry heart.

History
By the, a copy of Teachings of Loss: Light's Love could be found in abandoned and ruined secret temple to Shar underneath the town of Reithwin, the Gauntlet of Shar.

Appearances

Baldur's Gate III

## Teachings of Loss: Own Secret

Teachings of Loss: Own Secret was a Sharran text, printed before the 15 century DR.

Content
This tome was dedicated to secrecy—one of the most valued qualities among Sharrans. The tome stated that the Lady of Loss knew all the secrets, even those buried deep inside her worshipers' souls. She knew of pains, regrets, and suffering in their hearts. The tenets of the faith demanded that all her devotees be open with the goddess about their failings; in exchange, she offered soothing oblivion and freedom.

History
By the, a copy of Teachings of Loss: Own Secret could be found in the abandoned and ruined secret temple to Shar underneath the town of Reithwin, the Gauntlet of Shar.

## Telarantra

Telarantra was a War Wizard of Cormyr in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Personality
She was not above gloating when killing innocent people.

Abilities
One of her spells slew a fellow War Wizard almost immediately by stealing his lifeforce. Another of her spells broke a song dragon free from a magical effect that froze it in time; the same effect, or one not long after, caused a fiery explosion at a distance.

Relationships
Besides being a War Wizard, she worked for the Rightful Conspiracy.

History
Telarantra was one of the mages that arrived in the aftermath of the song dragon Ammaratha Cyndusk's attack on Vangerdahast at his hideout in Mouth o'Gargoyles in . The song dragon had been stopped with magic that froze it in time; as soon as she learned that, Telarantra immediately slew the nearest mage, and unfroze the dragon, unleashing an explosion. She expected the dragon would finish its task of slaying Vangerdahast.

However, Ammaratha Cyndusk chose to turn on her instead of slaying Vangerdahast, and quickly disemboweled her.

## Tent of the Bows and Quivers

The Tent of the Bows and Quivers, or nom-satakon erughe in Chuchian, was the palace compound that served as the headquarters of Chuqali Shilai, the qaghan of the Fankiang tribe, in the city of Li-Raz. It was considered the grandest building in all the Plain of Horses.

Description
The compound comprised four buildings made of red brick, each standing three stories high. Each had a different function. The first building was Shilai's living quarters. The second building housed meeting rooms and spaces for receiving visitors. The third building contained the qaghan's throne room and held his silver-plated treasures, with bows and quivers of arrows on one side and saddles and shields on the other. Finally, the fourth building stabled a herd of white camels, which were considered sacred, as well as the large white cart that transported Shilai's mobile personal yurt. The yurt itself was fashioned from camel hides sewn with silver thread.

## Teleport gem

A teleport gem was a device that teleported the user.

Powers
Teleport gems teleported the user to a predetermined location.

History
One such teleport gem was owned by Florin Falconhand in the mid-14 century DR; it was keyed to Evermeet, where the elven mother of his child lived.

Another such gem was owned by Queen Filfaeryl Obarskyr of Cormyr, who refused to use it to transport herself to safety during the ambush in Thundaerlyn Hall, in, at the hands of the Rightful Conspiracy. The gem was instead shattered by one of the conspirators while trying to skewer her with a blade.

The mage Carmanstos of Raven's Bluff created teleport gems of his own by imbuing enchantments in gemstones, which released spells when shattered. Another mage in Raven's Bluff, Aleesia, may have furnished a different kind of teleport gem to adventurers, possibly a silver orb which she may have named as a gem by mistake.The teleport gem is not referred to in the text again, and other references are instead to a silver orb that she provides instead, which is missing from the reference to the teleport gem.

## Temple of Filth

The Temple of Filth was a temple in the Shadowfell city of Evernight that was dedicated to Orcus, the demon prince of undeath, and his subordinate Doresain, patron of ghouls.

Location
The temple was located on the banks of the river of lava that poured through Evernight from Mount Hotenow. It sat in the same location as did the House of Knowledge in Neverwinter on the Material Plane.

A pall of death hung over the entire temple grounds. Even by the standards of the Shadowfell, it was a defiled place bursting with necromantic energies that sapped the strength of the living and empowered the undead. Lights were not as bright and the air was still and cold in comparison to the rest of Evernight.

Structure
The temple was ancient as of the, and by that time, it showed little evidence that it had once been a temple to both Bhaal and Myrkul. In its windows, rather than glass there was flesh that had been stretched so tightly that light could pass through it.

Interior
Bathed in the red-tinted light from the windows, the inside of the temple was dusty and adorned with holy symbols made of bone and flesh. The altar was composed of corpses on their hands and knees which had been sewn together. These corpses had to be frequently replaced as they rotted.

Located deep within the temple were locked doors that were trapped, cursed, and shrouded in magical darkness. Behind these doors was said to be the Pool of Daylight.

Activities
The Temple of Filth was a site of worship for Evernight's undead citizens, who would gather there to consume special flesh and chant profane litanies. Whenever a ghoul captured a living humanoid within Evernight, they would usually present them to the Temple for a "funeral" in which they were buried alive (to be eaten later) in a ceremony overseen by the temple priests.

Some residents of Evernight would seek out the priests to settle disputes, especially if they wished to avoid the harsh judgements of the city's courthouse, the House of Screams. In exchange for presiding over such matters, the priests generally demanded that all parties involved complete a service for the Temple, such as performing labor or capturing victims.

The ghouls of the temple were also charged with keeping the Pool of Daylight hidden and safeguarded.

History
As of the late 15 century DR, only the oldest of Evernight's undead remembered a time when the temple had been dedicated to Bhaal and Myrkul.

Inhabitants
As of the late 15 century DR, the temple was led by a high priest of Orcus known as Grand Disciple Ursuntos, who was the most politically powerful being in Evernight aside from the tribunal. He was fearfully served by several ghoul and vampire underpriests.

## Teeth (disambiguation)

"Teeth" was a popular name used for locations:
 Auril's Teeth, the northern tundra and glaciers of Faerûn,
 Dragon's Teeth, the strait between Koryo and Kozakura,
 Isle of Jagged Teeth, an island in the Sea of Fallen Stars,
 Omgar's Teeth, a maritime hazard near the Snout of Omgar in Samarach,
 Small Teeth, a mountain range in Amn,
 Sword's Teeth, the coastal cliffs of the Sword Coast,
 The Teeth (Trackless Sea), a cluster of small islands in the Trackless Sea,
 The Teeth (Great Sea), a group of islands in the Corsair Domains of Zakhara,
 Twin Teeth, the towers of the South Gate of Luskan,
 Vault of Gnashing Teeth, a cavern in the Underdark,
 Vishap's Teeth, a set of four pyramids in Zakhara, and
 Wood of Sharp Teeth, a large forest on the Sword Coast.

Some people and organizations used it in their names:
 Hupert Three-teeth, a master thief of Delzimmer,
 The Teeth (mercenaries), a mercenary company of ogres, and
 The Teeth of Calaunt, the army of Calaunt.

Various items also used it for metaphorical or literal intent:

 Ring of teeth, a magical ring,
 Teeth of Dahlver-Nar, a set of magical teeth,
 Teeth of Firemane, a set of magical darts, and
 Teeth Taker, a magical mace.

Some spells also used it in their names:
 Cone of teeth, an aquatic spell that gave water a cutting edge,
 Gauntlet of teeth, a spell that created whirling teeth, and
 Ice dragon's teeth, a spell that produced a burst of sharp ice.

## Tentfall

The tentfall was an experimental parachute conceived of by Nadul DaRoni while he developed other forms of powered flight.

Description
In addition to the heartland flier, air screw, and doubles wheel, DaRoni put some effort into a device that would allow a common person to fall safely from a great height. According to his claims, any person with enough canvas could make it, in the form of a  square and  tall pyramidal tent-like shape.This size doesn't seem to match the diagrams of the device, which would depict 12 feet instead. The tricky part was apparently holding onto the large ring that hung from beneath the tentfall as it drifted downwards.

Availability
Aurora's Whole Realms Catalogue listed the design by Nadul DaRoni, though it seemed to be a non-saleable item, instead intended to instruct others in the creation of their own tentfalls.

## Temple of Time

The Temple of Time was a temple to Amaunator founded in the ancient Netherese city of Trinity.

Description
The clergy of the Temple of Time had a much more relaxed and non-hierarchical set of doctrines compared to other sects in Netheril. Trinity had been founded by the remnants of three different Amaunatori groups, and in abandoning their theological differences they created a new and unified sect that cared little for the elaborate legalism and bureaucratic systems that were traditional among Amaunatoris.

History
The temple was built in  after lengthy construction efforts. The onset of the Shadowed Age in  brought with it omens of doom that invaded the dreams of the resident potentates, giving them visions of fires, storms, and darknesss. As the end of Netheril approached, the dreams spread to the wider population of the city, but they refused to leave.

The temple started shining with bright reflected light during the day in, creating a beacon that was visible for miles away, blinding passersby but also reinforcing the glory of Amaunator. This divine glow abruptly ended 100 years later during Karsus's Folly, while at the same time the sun was blocked by an eclipse, the sky filled with storms, and darkness fell upon the land. Within the next few hours the temple and Trinity had been abandoned as the population sought shelter to the south.

## Teralyn

Teralyn was a Netherese shadar-kai assassin who attacked the Cormyrean forces at the Wheloon prison in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Like other shadar-kai, Teralyn had ashy gray skin, black hair, facial tattoos and scarring. She was armed with two daggers and a spiked chain for weapons.

History

In the late 15 century DR, the forces of Returned Netheril attempted to take over the Cormyrean prison-town of Wheloon, using the population of Sharran cultists and innocents trapped within the urban prison. When the infamous Sharran priest Dedryk Black was recaptured and returned after escaping, he was interrogated in the Wheloon Docks Magistrate's Office by Magistrate Cornelius Latrimor and a band of heroes from the world of Eberron. After some questioning, Dedryk revealed that he and the other escapees had magically travelled through the shadows to freedom, but before he could tell more, Teralyn and her forces suddenly attacked the Wheloon Docks as a whole, threatening to overrun Cormyr's gateway to the prison.

Teralyn and her forces appeared seemingly from nowhere by using brief magical surges that temporarily overlapped the Shadowfell with material reality, allowing a small horde of living shadows to distract the defending Purple Dragons and War Wizards, and creating tangible shadowy barriers that split the defenders and kept them from rallying their forces properly. The attack was also joined by many furious and desperate prisoners, who were more than willing to fight for a chance at freedom and revenge. They even threatened to take a large cargo ship on the docks that had been used to move prisoners and soldiers into the city, but the attempted mass breakout was ultimately unsuccessful.

While Cormyr's forces were fighting for their lives, Teralyn simply stepped through the shadows into the chambers where Dedryk Black was being kept, slew Magistrate Cornelius Latrimor, and sent Dedryk back through the Shadowfell to freedom. Before she could abscond herself, she was cornered by the heroes from Eberron and slain in turn, with only her last orders recovered as explanation.

Appearances

## Temple of Mystra (King's Forest)

The Temple of Mystra was a great Cormyrean cathedral dedicated to the Mother of All Magic, the goddess Mystra, located in the King's Forest near the town of Eveningstar. It was built sometime in the late 14 century DR, and abandoned after Mystra's death and the Spellplague that followed, after which it was claimed as a base by drow.The Temple of Mystra in Wheloon, though proved false, was intended to be the first temple to Mystra in Cormyr. It was built in 1374 DR, so the Temple of Mystra in Eveningstar must have been built between then and Mystra's death in 1385 DR.

Location
The temple was built under the tree canopies, close to the northern edge of the King's Forest, on the western shores of the Starwater River south of Eveningstar.

Description

The temple was built both above and below ground, above and below a large hill. By the late 15 century DR, the above-ground structure was mostly crumbled and ruined, and the underground was partially collapsed. The hillside entrance opened into a large columned atrium with a waterfall trickling water into the reflecting pool at its center, and from there the temple split into hallways, all leading into different parts of the complex. One path led to an altar to Mystra with glowing silver stars still shining down on the chamber even after the deity's demise. Another path led to a library of texts on the Arcane. And another one opened up into a cathedral's main ceremonial hall. The latter ruins were outfitted with extensive slave pens and corrals, as well as various fortifications, gates, and spell wards to offer the drow base protection against do-gooders and prevent escapes.

History

The Temple of Mystra was abandoned after the goddess' demise in the, and was left ruined and forgotten until the drow threat rose in the . Although most of Lolth's schemes were focused elsewhere, the Spider Queen Lolth had her eyes on the small community of Eveningstar, and it was targeted by drow raiders from the Underdark city of Sschindylryn. Members of the drow houses Szind and Avithoul used the ruins of the Temple of Mystra as a base from which to capture slaves from Eveningstar, including commoners, War Wizards, and Purple Dragons alike, and shackled them with enchanted slave collars. Apart from capturing slaves, the drow were tasked by their goddess with finding the "Thread of the Weave," a fragment of the Weave torn from Mystra in the Spellplague.

Unaware of the threat, a band of Evenor youngsters made up of Cormul Haythorne, Torin Betrich, and Ana Brabener decided that the "Thread of the Weave" was surely hidden in the ruined temple and ventured out, only to be attacked by drow slavers, with Ana being taken and Cormul and Torin escaping. During her capture, young Ana showed an impressive magical gift, piquing the interest of her new masters.

While Torin stayed look for her, Cormul returned to Eveningstar and told Elminster and a band of adventurers from the world of Eberron of the capture. With the Old Sage's blessing, the outworlders set out to rescue the captured Ana and slay the slavers. When they arrived, they were attacked by the magically controlled folk of Eveningstar, driven by their slave masters, though they were able to be freed. Along the way in, they encountered the drow wizard Eldrin Szind, who was busy looting the temple and took a moment to realize that he was under attack. Though he used magic scrolls from the abandoned library to summon elementals, he was defeated all the same.

After much combat, the adventurers came across a chamber where Ana Brabener was being interrogated over the magics she had displayed during her capture. Her captor Odezzt Avithoul sent Ana and other slaves into combat against her rescuers, but the foul slavemaster was slain nevertheless. After the fight, Ana's horror at the situation let her break out of her own collar, much to her shock. The heroes and Ana then had to fight through a great many drow to escape the temple, with Ana using her strange gift to break the magical barriers that were being thrown up to trap them. Finally, the drider slaver Phargred Avithoul attempted to block their escape, but was cut down in the process.

Among the treasures recovered from the temple were an archaic device that functioned like a rune arm, and an ancient gemstone that could be used as a docent, both strange and esoteric magical items.

## Tenthar Taerhamoos

Tenthar Taerhamoos was an archmage in the mid–-8 century DR.

Description
He was a tall man, and wore a black cloak, with rich robes underneath and a gem-studded medallion.

Relationships
He had a years-long enmity with the mage Umbregard. He was served by many golems. He was also known by sight to Elminster Aumar.

Abilities
He could cast chain lightning, and a spell akin to teleport, as well as some spell that granted him the ability to phase through materials. He could also cast a spelltale magic that warned him if anyone crossed a given portal. Another of his spells allowed him to hurl a deadly sphere of purple flames.

Possessions
He had an enchanted medallion he could use to steady and augment his spells. He was the master of Phoenix Tower. He was also carrying a wand of some sort.

History
At the start of the Chaos of the Art in spring of the, Tenthar Taerhamoos barricaded himself in Phoenix Tower, and had his golems dig trenches right until they decayed into nothing.

About two tendays later, Tenthar Taerhamoos sought out the counsel of Kadeln Parosper in the House of the Ladystar, which had its gates closed, as a mob had formed just outside. Unable to cross the gates with his spells, he learned only that the priests of Mystra knew no more than he did about the Art, and was attacked by the mob; he retaliated with chain lightning before teleporting away. Back at his home, he teleported to the forest near Starmantle after learning someone had used a portal near Tangletrees to go there, suspecting the elves were on the move. He brought with himself a wedge of cheese.

At the woods near Starmantle, in what was then the Dead Place, he ambushed a number of Sharran Dreadspells who were distracted fighting the elven wizard Starsunder, and Tenthar's rival of many years, Umbregard of Galadorna. His deadly spell appeared to cause some feedback, causing the death of Dread Sister Avroana all the way back in the House of Holy Night. They later shared a drink with Elminster Aumar, a Chosen of Mystra, who came to heal them on Mystra's behalf.

## Temporal shell

Temporal shell was a powerful chronomancy spell that duplicated an innate power of royal time dimensionals to create a timeless bubble around the caster. (Time dimensionals were creatures known to serve the elven deity Labelas Enoreth.)

Effects
This spell created an immovable shell with a  radius around the caster, protecting from the effects of time outside the shell. That is, within the time shell, the caster could function in time, while outside the bubble, time had effectively stopped. (This spell differed from the slightly weaker time stop spell in that time within the area of effect stopped flowing for all but the caster when a time stop spell was cast. For the temporal shell spell, time stopped flowing everywhere except for within the area of effect.) Anyone within the temporal shell with the caster was also able to move in time. Anyone leaving the shell would immediately stop moving in time, except for the caster, whose departure would instantly dispel the shell and its effects.

The spell took one minute to cast and lasted at least a couple hours (relative to the caster's flow of time within the bubble.)

Components
The spell required verbal, somatic, and material components, the latter being a complex and expensive device that required advanced preparation. A diamond valued at at least 5,000 gold pieces had to first be enchanted with a temporal stasis spell. The diamond must then be placed within a crystal ball of at least 1,000 gold pieces in value. Finally, the encapsulated diamond had to be physically touched by a time dimensional while in the Demiplane of Time. Casting of the spell fully consumed the prepared diamond and its crystal globe.

## Tekkil

Tekkil was a plant found in Faerûn that was used as a drug.

Tekkil was a succulent plant found in swamps that possessed fat red leaves containing milky sap.

Usage
Tekkil leaves were chewed to release the milky juice inside of them. This juice acted as a painkiller but also caused lethargy and stupor. A dose of tekkil was valued at 5 gold pieces.

## Temple of Tempus (Bezantur)

The Temple of Tempus in Bezantur, Thay was a place of worship dedicated to Tempus, a god who was popular with the locals owing to his association with unrestrained violence.

Location
The temple was located in south-central Bezantur, the so-called "City of a Thousand Temples". Its immediate neighbors included the Temple of Sune to the west, the House of Cyric and the Temple of Bane to the north, the Temple of Tyr to the east, and the temples of Leira and Myrkul to the south.

Structure
The temple itself was a fortress of granite with the symbol of Tempus—a fiery sword on a red field—displayed prominently above the main gate. This gate was constructed of iron and was flanked by two enormous statues of Tempus: the statue on the right depicted him astride his black steed Deiros while the one on the left rode his white steed Veiros.

History
As of the mid-to-late 14 century DR, Tempus remained a popular deity in Bezantur despite the fact that the church of Tempus was often at odds with the Red Wizards of Thay. The church's representative at the Temple during this time was Priest-Captain Zeidero.

## Temple of Ghaunadaur

The Temple of Ghaunadaur was a temple in the Sword Coast North dedicated to the worship of Ghaunadaur.

Location
The temple was located on the north-eastern side of the Sword Mountains, near Wyvern Tor.

Description
The Temple was described as foul, with walls and floors that seemed to take on qualities of ooze, and prayers to Ghaunadaur lining the walls.

History
Sometime around 1490 DR, the Champion of the Burning Dawn traveled to the temple to recover the Sigil of Ghaunadaur for the drow Morlesh, where, after fighting though the many oozes of the dungeon as well as encountering a number of cultists of Ghaunadaur, they confronted Morlesh in the temple's innermost chambers, as he explained his wish to rebuild the cult with himself as leader and intending to use mind control to offer his deity willing human sacrifices.

## Temple of Kossuth (Escalant)

The Temple of Kossuth in Escalant, Thay, was a place of worship dedicated to the fire god Kossuth. It concealed a secret portal to the Elemental Plane of Fire, known as the Portal of Fire.

Interior
A vault inside the temple contained the permanent one-way Portal of Fire leading to the Plane of Fire. The portal was a  stone archway that was always aflame, an effect achieved via a clever system of small tubes within the stone through which slow-burning alchemist's fire was regularly piped to the arch's surface.

History
The portal was created during the Salamander War in the, in a joint effort between the Red Wizard Aznar Thrul and the Kossuthan clergy of the temple. After successfully beseeching Kossuth to send fire elementals to drive back Thay's salamander foes, Thrul arranged for this portal to be created so that the salamanders could be forced back to their home plane. The existence of the portal was kept a secret, at least in part because Thrul hoped to one day find a means to use it to his advantage against his rivals by creating a return portal. Thrul was able to monitor the portal's use, and was aware if anyone passed through it. Meanwhile, the banished salamanders remained bitter, and would slay anyone who dared cross the threshold into their home plane.

As of, Thrul recognized that he would need to expel the Kossuthans and seize control of the temple if he wished to realize his ambition of creating a two-way portal. However, the existence of the portal was no longer a secret by this time, and tales and rumors about it had spread throughout Escalant. Such rumors often misunderstood the purpose of the portal, and frequently speculated that it allowed creatures from the Plane of Fire to travel into the Prime Material plane (whether on behalf of Kossuth or on behalf of Thay's salamander enemies). Still other tales had begun to suggest that the portal did not lead to the Plane of Fire at all, but instead to a secret treasury of dark Red Wizard secrets.

Inhabitants
The temple's Kossuthan priests were tasked with defending the Portal of Fire. However, they were amenable to negotiating access to the portal for those looking to sneak out of Escalant, and were especially willing to help followers of their god or his allies. These priests were also known to enjoy a trip to the taverns, and were thus responsible for creating some of the rumors about the portal.

## Tearah Initoo

Tearah Initoo was the Master of the Market in Port Nyanzaru, Chult, during the late 15 century DR.

Personality
She was very proud of Port Nyanzaru's long history of trade and crafting.

Activities
As Master of the Market, Initoo and her associates oversaw all trade within Port Nyanzaru's Bazaar and ensured that the market's needs were met. She also worked alongside local merchant consortiums and craftspeople to enforce the monopolies of the merchant princes and ensure payment of taxes. In addition, she coordinated special orders from important clients, matching them to approved merchants or craftspeople.

Foreign merchants and craftspeople wanting to sell their wares in Port Nyanzaru were directed to speak with the Master of the Market, who could connect them to agents of the merchant prince who controlled the trade and sale of the relevant goods. For instance, alchemists and those dealing in alchemical supplies or potions might be directed to Giregi Igiri, while smiths and weapon merchants might be directed to Silaha Mnunzi.

History

During the events of the death curse, Tearah received special orders from the likes of the wealthy heiress Teyileri Siwifi (seeking to buy a magnificent necklace), the guard captain Uponga Naodha (seeking to acquire foreign titansteel armor), the huntress Mirea Sain (seeking a leatherworker to make gear for the Hunters' Guild of Chult), the artificer Chonigi Tomi (seeking objects touched by the Far Realm), and the foreign merchant Sofia Chiaroscuro (hoping to reclaim her confiscated goods).

Teth'ka's people, the gith group that would become Crèche Y'llek, attacked and claimed the Rosymorn Monastery of Lathander in the Western Heartlands of West Faerûn sometime before the . Soon after, the function of Crèche was changed as the githyanki war against the mind flayers in Faerûn shifted in balance. Teth'ka, loyal to her Kith'tak, was a warrior in training who was to become a sarth, leading her kin into battle. ## Teyileri Siwifi

Teyileri Siwifi was a wealthy young woman in Port Nyanzaru, Chult, during the late 15 century DR.

Personality
She had a bold sense of style and sought to impress those to whom she spoke, if not stun them into silence.

Relationships
Her father was a close associate of the merchant prince Wakanga O'tamu, and she was known to spend time at the prince's villa to enjoy his menagerie.

History
During the events of the death curse, Teyileri was embarrassed while giving a toast when a man rudely interrupted her. Seeking to make sure it did not happen again, her best solution was to acquire three magnificent necklaces—fashioned from rubellite, silverspruce, and sphene—believing that wearing such items would clearly convey her importance. She therefore contacted the Master of the Market to commission the pieces.

## Temple of Shar (Wheloon)

The Temple of Shar in Wheloon was a center of Sharran worship, controlled by the Darklady Storna Harnwood, and headquarters of the Night Brigade gang during the late 15 century DR.

Structure

The temple was an expansive, fortified, well-built structure. Its foundations were built atop the ruins of the former homes of the Riverside Rats, and the rubble and remains of their existence could still be found in the lower levels.

One chamber comprised a deep pit filled with garbage and covered with shadows, and the flock were told that leaping into it would lead to the sweet embrace of oblivion. Instead, they would simply add their corpses to the hidden heap of refuse.

In Storna's "treasury", she kept the loot that she had won over years of service but considered it of little worth compared to her divine service. She instead left it with living shadows, which "played" with her heaps of coins.

Activities
Storna used the temple to sway and recruit Wheloon prisoners, increasing her power and that of the gang. In addition to her regular living followers, she was aided and protected by umbral gargoyles and other shadow monstrosities.

History

The temple was built atop the homes of the Riverside Rats gang, and became a symbol of the Night Brigade's power. When the Rats finally set up a counter-offensive, they began with the lesser officers of the gang, taking out Battlemaster Agarta the Breaker, Master Thief Karissa the Knife, and Grand Arcanist Darstil Vurusk, who each controlled an area of expertise in the gang. Realising the incoming danger, Storna had the Temple of Shar locked up, preventing any visitors or invaders from entering. On their approach the Rat's allies found the Selûnite Brunwell Godber outside the temple, who gave auspicious moon-related advice and promised that Selûne would make herself known. They then used their rat talismans to bypass the defenses and sneak into the temple, cutting through the defenders as they went. They encountered multiple opportunities to banish Shar's darkness along the way, until by the end the temple was well-lit by Selûne's light. Storna was found and fought, and accepted her end as one of Shar's humble servants. Without Storna as their leader, the Night Brigade assuredly crumbled, sparing Wheloon their torments and allowing the Riverside Rats to return to their homes.

## Telepath

A telepath, also known as a telepathic psion, was a type of psion who specialized in the telepathy discipline of psionics, honing their skills in mental communication, mind reading, and manipulating other creatures. They were equivalent to the enchanters of arcane magic.

Culture
Like other forms of psion, telepaths could arise from almost any race, including blue goblins, changelings, eladrin, and gnomes. The path of the telepath was also particularly prevalent among mind flayers, ulitharids, and elder brains.

In the city of Skullport, telepaths were prevalent among the psions trained at the Hall of Mental Splendor to become cognition thieves.

Out of all psions, telepaths were particularly wary of undead, as their immunity to mental effects rendered most of a telepath's arsenal of abilities ineffective.

Abilities
Telepaths almost exclusively utilized powers that fell within the purview of the telepathy discipline. Their specialization in this discipline allowed telepaths to unlock a suite of powers that were unknown to other psions, such as brain lock, psionic dominate, schism, and mind seed. In the years following the Spellplague, almost all telepathic psions exhibited the powers distract and send thoughts.

Their discipline lent telepaths to influencing and manipulating the minds of others, though they could just as well deceive their senses or unleash devastating mental assaults to destroy an opponent's mind.

Telepaths were also skilled in the art of remote viewing, a psionic means of scrying.

Possessions
While traditionally psionics users such as telepathic psions didn't need any physical tools or weaponry to practice their discipline, following the Spellplague they began using implements to enhance their abilities. Typically these took the form of orbs and staves, through which they would channel their psionic energy into powers.

Notable Telepaths
 Encephalithid, an elder brain that ruled the mindflayer city of Oryndoll.
 Gryxt, a blue goblin telepath that ruled the warrens of Brikklext from behind the scenes alongside the nomad Driklret.
 Urdol, an aboleth telepath that ruled the aboleth city of Dupapn.
 Zelia, a yuan-ti telepath who planted a mind seed in the mind of the young psion Arvin.

## Tethyamar Mining Outpost

The Tethyamar mining outpost was one of many outlying bases on the southern edge of the mines of Tethyamar, which found use again in the 15 century DR by the expedition of Hal Stoutheart that sought to exploit the jewels of ardor.

Description

The outpost was placed inside a spacious cavern that also contained a small waterfall and pool. A variety of structures were carved into the walls of the cavern, including storage for supplies, a stable for oxen, and a small tavern and eatery. In the middle of the outpost was a tower or keep that nearly reached the ceiling, from which Hal Stoutheart addressed the assembled dwarves with fiery, optimistic speeches.

The outpost sat at an intersection of many tunnels and shafts, giving it access to a wide area underground. These locations included Mineshaft 214S, Ironvale Deep, the expansive Eastern Reaches and Blue Stone Cavern, and a nearby hidden drow stronghold.

History

The expedition to mine the jewels of ardor and reclaim Tethyamar began with the demon lord Graz'zt, who took the identity of the long-dead Hal Stoutheart for himself. Posing as the explorer, he gathered up interested dwarves by sharing tales of legendary riches and beguiling them with subtle demonic magics to stoke their greed and ambition. He intended to lure them into Tethyamar so that they would either be destroyed or dragged down to his level.

Once they had enough numbers, the expedition set off for Tethyamar and began enthusiastically rebuilding the outpost while searching for the jewels of ardor. Arman Argentine went to Eveningstar and worked as a recruiter, showing off samples of the jewels of ardor to entice the locals. One group of adventurers agreed to take a chance on the expedition, traveling the hundreds of miles from Cormyr to Tethyamar. At the same time, House Argith of Sschindylryn launched their own effort into Tethyamar, seeking wealth and glory for themselves by attacking and enslaving the dwarves.

The elder Asta the Deep-Minded followed her nephew to Tethyamar and kept an aloof and critical eye on the expedition, well aware of the strange behavior going on. When Arman's recruits arrived, she wrangled the surfacers into helping her help the others, by keeping the dwarves alive and gathering more information about what was really going on. She had them work with Hagen Silverthumb, Eluf the Steadfast, Gunhild the Watchful, and Freygar Plumbline as they struggled against the Argith drow and their own natures. Finally, Inger Ingersdottir reported that Hal had gone missing in the Eastern Reaches, and sent the team after him. Instead of the expedition leader, they found a gauntlet of challenges including the illusion of Faraway, all created by the revealed Graz'zt. After a cursory battle, the demon lord abandoned his schemes and departed to parts unknown.

With the revelation of Graz'zt's manipulations and the end of the jewel's influence, the dwarves were left stunned and confused, unsure what to do without the grand goal they had driven themselves into a frenzy for.

Inhabitants

The prominent residents of the outpost included:
 Hal Stoutheart, leader of the expedition (actually Graz'zt in disguise).
 Inger Ingersdottir, Hal's assistant and manager of the outpost.
 Gunhild the Watchful, the guard captain.
 Freygar Plumbline, a mining surveyor.
 Egil Hakon, a mining foreman and cleric.
 Eluf the Steadfast, a mining foreman.
 Rielle Proudforge, a miner.
 Radagor Redston, a general trader.
 Sylvi Greenglas, the bartender.
 Asta the Deep-Minded, a concerned elder.
 Mirryn Vale, an elven priest of Amaunator.

## Teldin Moore

Teldin Moore was a human farmer and former soldier from the world of Krynn. After a spelljammer crashed into his farmhouse, he was given a magical cloak by a dying captain Hemar, thus becoming the Cloakmaster.

Description

Personality
A simple fellow, he was likeable with a strong sense of morality, a wry sense of humor, and brave without being fearless.

Abilities

Possessions
The eponymous magical cloak of the Cloakmaster.

Activities

Relationships

History
Teldin Moore was a tough human farmer who, as a young man, ran away from his overbearing father. He went in search of glory, and ran straight into the War of the Lance. All he found however was misery, while knights and heroes were out jabbing at dragons with pointy sticks, he was hacking up their frozen corpses and burning the meat, and digging mass graves for all their victims. When he finally returned home, he resolved to live a life of simple farm work.

Teldin witnessed the wreckage of a flying wooden longship fall out of the sky and demolish the farm that his grandfather and father had built. Initially believing it to be a dragon or a demon from the Abyss, Teldin soon realised that it was a vehicle and set about searching for survivors. He found only one; an elven woman. He tried to free her from the debris she was trapped under, but her long cloak obstructed his attempts, and he couldn't seem to open its silver clasp.

The elf woke up and started babbling in Elvish, which he only understood after she placed a finger to his lips and seemingly cast a spell. She cryptically told him to take the cloak she wore to its creators and keep it away from the neogi. She then easily opened the clasp and affixed the cloak around Teldin's neck, then perished from her wounds.

However another had survived the crash; a huge man with the head of a hippo, who wielded some kind of metal wand. This person introduced himself by pulling a trigger on the wand, but the device backfired, exploded, and knocked the hippo-man out with the recoil. Utterly confused, Teldin tied the hippo-man up and began searching the burning wreckage for other survivors. He found only dead bodies, but noted with interest a large chair in one of the vehicle's rooms that seems completely undamaged by the fire, with a white-robed wizard slumped nearby.

The hippo-man soon woke up, and Teldin quickly cleared up the misunderstanding that he did not in fact kill the captain and stole her cloak. The hipo-man introduced himself "Private Herphan Gomja of the Noble Giff" and the two established an uneasy alliance. Gomja explained that his crew were sailing across the stars before they were attacked by the neogi, evil spider aliens. The Neogi seemed to want the captain's cloak, which she had described as "her advantage", but Gomja did not know any more than that. He went on telling Teldin about the giff, that they enter military service from a young age, and want nothing more than glory or death in battle. Teldin, whose experiences of war were much less grandiose, disagreed with this outlook on life, but they both agreed to work together for the time being.

Soon the neogi showed up to investigate the crash site, accompanied by their "lordservants"; big pincery monsters with hypno-eyes who were later named as umber hulks. With his farm gone, Teldin decided to travel north to the city of Kalaman, where he had family who could lend him money to start rebuilding. He would also need to find a way to remove the captain's cloak, as no matter what he did the clasp would not open. Helpfully the cloak shrunk near water, allowing him to take a bath and being far less obvious.

Arriving in Kalaman, Teldin visited a dwarven merchant attempting to cut the clasp on the cloak, but it failed spectacularly, releasing a burst of energy that knocked everyone on their backs. Worse still, Teldin's family had left town years ago, leaving him without financial aid. While in town, many who saw Gomja the giff would stare in terror as most believed him to be either a monster from the Abyss or even worse some kind of draconian experiment. Nearing his wit's end, Teldin finally found some good fortune when a mercenary leader recognised him from back in the War. Named Vandoorm he was a mentor and father figure to Teldin. After a night spent drinking and swapping stories, he offered Teldin and Gomja a place on his crew. They decided to join up and travel to Palanthas, where there was said to be a sage who knew everything. Surely he would know how to remove the cloak, and how to get Gomja off the fearful world.

## Telepathy (arcane spell)

Telepathy was an evocation spell that allowed a wizard to create a telepathic link with another creature, allowing for two-way mental communication.

Effects
For a duration of twenty-four hours, the spellcaster and an affected creature could instantaneously transmit words, sounds, images, and other sensory messages to one another. Whenever the wizard did so, the target would recognize them as being the one telepathically communicating with them. Additionally, creatures with an animal-level of intelligence targeted by the spell would be me capable of understanding the words or other sensory messages being relayed to them by the caster.

Telepathy had an unlimited casting range, though wizards could only target creatures that were willing and that they had previously met at some point. If either party traveled to another plane of existence the spell would end prematurely.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required a material component in the form of a pair of linked silver rings.

Notable Users
 Arcturia, who inscribed the spell within her personal spellbook, Arcturia's Arcane Esoterica: Vol. IX.
 Canthan Doolittle.

Appendix
See also

 Telepathic broadcast
 Rary's telepathic bond

## Telepathy (divine spell)

Telepathy was a divine spell of the thought sphere that allowed a cleric to create a telepathic link with another creature, allowing for two-way mental communication.

Effects
Regardless of whether the two shared a language, the spellcaster and affected creature could mentally communicate with each other at approximately four times the speed that verbal communication took. Both participants could only "hear" thoughts that were purposely being sent. The spell did not give access to other thoughts, nor emotions or memories.

Telepathy had a casting range of . In order for the spell to take effect, a target had to be a creature with at least a "semi-" level of intelligence. Additionally, unwilling targets could be affected by this spell, though they had a chance to resist the casting.

With multiple casting, more than one telepathic channels could be active at once, though they would not function as a network. This meant that targeted creatures could only telepathically communicate the caster, not with each other. These telepathic links could not persist past the boundary of a crystal sphere, but worked without issue underwater.

Components
Casting this spell required only verbal and somatic components.

Notable Users
Telepathy was contained within the pages of the holy books Scepter of Mystra, Tome of Torment, and Wythyndle's Round Book.

## Telempathic projection

Telempathic projection was a psionic power of the telepathy discipline that allowed a manifester to project emotions into the minds of other creatures and thereby subtly alter their moods.

Effects
Telempathic projection allowed a manifester to project emotions upon anyone within a  diameter of them whose minds had been previously contacted. Typically this power could not radically change an emotional state, such as making an angry individual happy or turning love into hate, only adjusting them by a degree. However, on rare occasions, manifestations of the power did exhibit this capacity for drastic alteration. If a manifestation of telempathic projection backfired, all affected individuals would be stricken with strong negative emotions towards the manifester for the following 1 to 6 minutes.

Prior to the Time of Troubles,The loss and stabilization of psionics mentioned in Forgotten Realms Adventures and The Complete Psionics Handbook are in-universe references to the initial lack of psionics in 2 edition AD&D, and the reintroduction of psionics with a new ruleset, respectively. Though this in-universe explanation is somewhat contradicted by the use of psionics set in Toril's past, such as Netheril: Empire of Magic. the power operated in a much shorter directional path, targeting those whom the manifester was facing.

In the late 14 century DR, telempathic projection could only target one creature at a time and had a targeting range of, plus  or more depending upon the skill of the manifester. It was still limited to altering a target's emotional state by one step. It primarily used for the purpose of adjusting a creatures' attitudes towards the manifester in a positive direction,

Prerequisite Powers
Older versions of telempathic projection required knowing the powers contact and mindlink.

Displays
When used this power manifested a visual display.

Users
This power was knowable by psions and wilders, as well as ardents and divine minds that followed the communication mantle. And while it typically wasn't found to be a wild talent, some psionically gifted necromancers exhibited it as such, as they were known for developing unusual mental abilities.

Telempathic projection was exhibited as an innate psionic ability by adult intellect devourers, lythlyx, and ustilagors.

In psionics terms, telempathic projection was also known by agathinon.

Known/Notable Users
 Raulothim, a great wyrm emerald dragon.

## Telepathy (discipline)

The telepathy discipline of psionics revolved around making contact with and affecting the minds of other sentient creatures, influencing or controlling their behavior. Such powers could allow a manifester to read minds, erase memories, instigate doubt towards one's senses, swap personalities, or spy on targets from afar. Some of the most representative powers of this discipline included aversion, psionic charm, and psionic dominate, as well as the psionic attack and defense modes. Its equivalent among the schools of magic was enchantment. Much like the enchantment school, telepathy had two subdisciplines called Charm and Compulsion.

Description
Using any telepathic power required making "contact" with a target's mind, which some referred to as being its own specific power.The nature of "contact" has varied significantly in each edition and version of psionics, with it being unclear as to whether mentions of "contact" are referring to the use of a power or proficiency within the mechanics and which are just basic descriptions. See the contact page for more information. Some even claimed that this contact power was necessary for using the psionic defense modes.In its initial printing, The Complete Psionics Handbook listed contact as a prerequisite power for the psionic defense modes.  Subsequent printings changed this, clarifying that defense modes required no prerequisite powers. Many telepathic powers also had the advantage of not needing to be within line of sight of a target.

Subdisciplines
Charm Powers that altered the way a target regarded the manifester, typically making them view the user as a good friend.
Compulsion Powers that force an affected creature to act in some manner beyond their natural will or alter the way in which their minds work. Powers under this subdiscipline ranged from granting ongoing control over a subject, instigating actions or changes to a subject's mental state, or powers that allowed a manifester to determine the actions that subject's are compelled to do.

Reputation
Telepathy and its powers were often stereotyped, in the eyes of the average commoner and others less familiar with psionics, as the heart of what psionics were all about.

Notable Users
 Telepaths, psions who chose to specialize in mastery of telepathy over all other disciplines. Their specialization in the discipline lead them to accessing telepathy powers that were largely unobtainable by other psionics users.
 Among the various forms of psionics users, wilders were notable for struggling to use telepathy powers. This was due to their temperamental personalities. This difficulty would further worsen as a wilder's mastery of psionic powers grew.
 For Athasian thri-kreen, telepathy was the most common primary discipline among chakak (psionicists) and tekchakak (pack psionicists), as it aided in group coordination and communication within the clutch. However, the discipline's powers were of limited use to Athasian thri-kreen when it came to combat, due to the considerable differences between the kreen and non-kreen minds.
 Hrodel, the crystal dragon thane of Sardior, whose area of focus was telepathy.

## Templar's bastion

Templar's bastion was a kind of mithral plate armor found in Eberron, used by the Templars of the Church of the Silver Flame.

Powers
Templar's bastion armor was enhanced with extra protection against both physical and magical threats, though unholy weapons cut through it more easily. Those that struck the wearer were hit with blast of divine light in retribution.

History
These suits were said to have been blessed by Tira Miron, and were worn by Templars Silver Flame. This armor was awarded to adventurers who fought the Overlord Spinner of Shadows before her escape from the Spinner's Prison.

## Temple of Helm (Helmsport)

The Temple of Helm in Helmsport was the first place of worship of Helm the Vigilant One in Maztica, erected in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

History
Sometime in Helmsport's early years, the settlement became the target of attacks by the red wolves of Payit. These cunning beasts took the lives of a dozen builders among those who worked on the Temple of Helm, before the colonial authorities assembled a group of hunters to kill the pack's alpha. The hunt was successful, and the aptly named leader of the attacks, the Red Wolf of Payit, was taxidermied and proudly displayed in the finished temple. The stuffed beast caused an uproar among the Payitlans, who thought it disrespectful to the killed creature. Soon after, a chain of maladies afflicted the temple's inhabitants, leading the Payit folk to whisper of the Red Wolf's angry spirit punishing its killers. Helmites and settlers considered it to be nothing but superstition. Their opinions started to waver after the temple's High Priest perished of a heartstop right by the display of the stuffed wolf. After some unrest, the stuffed animal was removed and sent to Amn to calm the superstitious.

## Tear of Ubtao

Tears of Ubtao was a type of sap extracted from the bronzewood trees believed to had been blessed by the deity Ubtao of Chult.

Description
Tears of Ubtao were gathered from bronzewood that lived and thrived in the depths of the deadly tomb of the foul lich Acererak. The locals believed that the trees survived thanks to the jungle god's blessings.

Tears of Ubtao were a valuable substance widely used in masterwork crafting. It could be mixed with lakh varnish to create magical living varnish. In its pure form, Tears of Ubtao could be used to treat bronzewood lumber.

History
In the late 15 century DR, Tears of Ubtao were harvested in Chult by and for the masterwork artisans of Port Nyanzaru by the adventurers of Neverwinter, during the death curse crisis.

## Tenth Serôs War

The Tenth Serôs War was the tenth most major conflict in Serôs, the undersea realms of the Sea of Fallen Stars, centered around the fracturing and eventual dissolution of the merfolk empire of Hmurrath. This warring period lasted for sixty-one years, from  to .It is erroneously stated on pages 62 & 139 of Sea of Fallen Stars that it was the Fifth Serôs War where the kingdom fell apart, whereas in other parts of the book it is clearly stated to have been the Tenth Serôs War. The latter conflict is also the one cited by the novel The Sea Devil's Eye.

Location
A good deal of the conflict unfolded on the Hmur Plateau,It is not explicitly stated where fighting during the Tenth War took place. However, seeing as Hmurrath's territory encompassed the plateau and it is stated that "an ixitxachitl baron’s plan to take over the plateau" was thwarted, one can infer that the Hmur Plateau was likely a central point in the conflict. which for centuries had been controlled by Hmurrath, though some fighting also took place to the west on the Haunted Plains, beneath the Bay of Yhaunn.

History
Causes
The empire of Hmurrath had long been ruled by kings and queens, referred to in the Merfolk language as 'Praessar', but in the 8 century DR the rightful rulership over the empire fell to six living princes. With Hmurrath having long since fallen into corrupt despotism, it was not long before long the princes squabbled amongst one another and broke out into conflict, beginning the Tenth War in .
Battles
More than just one conflict between two opposing forces, this Serôs War was comprised of a series of seven separate civil wars within Hmurrath that ended up tearing the empire apart, pitting nearly all of the aquatic races against one other and causing devastating losses of life. From the likes of merfolk against merfolk, merfolk against shalarin, and shalarin against sea elves.

The conflicts tearing Hmurrath apart slowly waned over the next decade. By, it seemed as though the warring might soon be coming to an end. However, that year Serôs experienced a large influx of refugee clans from the surface elf empire of Cormanthyr's capital Myth Drannor, which had fallen a year prior to the Army of Darkness in the Weeping War. Using an artifact known as the Sashelan Glass, these surface elves transformed themselves into sea elves. This influx of elves was quickly met with backlash, stirring up the second half of the Tenth Serôs War,It stated on page 136 of Sea of Fallen Stars that Myth Drannan refugees "stirred up the latter half of the Seventh Serôs War." However, that war occurred from -108 DR to -106 DR and the city of Myth Drannor wasn't on a downward trajectory until several centuries later. The Tenth Serôs War is far more likely to be the conflict that was prolonged by the arrival of Myth Drannan refugees, seeing as it coincides with both the Weeping War, which ended in 714 DR, and the documented refugee influx of 715 DR. In the case of the latter, it is stated on page 70 of Sea of Fallen Stars that "These Hmurran civil wars might have died down earlier but the interjection of Myth Drannan refugees..." with the other aquatic races of Hmurrath concerned that they were trying to restore their ancient empire, Aryselmalyr.

In, a sahuagin princess of Aleaxtis by the named of T'rysskar received a vision from her peoples' patron god, Sekolah, prompting the sahuagin to attack the ancient city of Myth Nantar. When the raiding parties came, they were accompanied by siege crabs, a form of living siege weapon unseen in the depths of Serôs prior. With its primary defenders, the Dukars, absent for the last three years Myth Nantar suffered considerable damage by these siege crabs before their sahuagin masters moved in to slaughter the city's remaining merfolk and sea elven defenders. But it was not long before the sahuagin abandoned the city, due to its mythal invoking discomfort in them.

In, beyond the notice of other aquatic sentients amidst all the conflict, a group of sea elves cleared and resettle a portion of the cliffs above the Haunted Plains, founding the kingdom of Keryvyr.

 was considered by some to be the date when Hmurrath had fallen completely,As the date of 724 DR is also given on the same page where it's erroneously stated that Hmurrath fell during the Fifth Serôs War, it's not out of the realm of possibility that the date might also be an error and therefore that the end date of the Tenth Serôs War (750 DR) might be the more accurate one. with merfolk left overwrought with grief over its fracturing. Under the guidance of Selana the Peacemaker, a merfolk oracle blessed by deities of both the sea and the land, many of the surviving clans reformed around some of their older settlements and eventually founded the state of Eadraal in .

This new faction of merfolks, based in the city of Voalidru, were driven by a noble desire to restore peace to the undersea lands of Serôs, as well as peace between the merfolk and other aquatic races. However, remnants of the empire would continue to wage war for the next fifteen years. These last warring remnants would finally be destroyed by, marking the end of the Tenth Serôs War.

Aftermath
The end of this war would be marked by Serôsian historians as the end of Serôs's Third epoch, the Time of Merfolk, and the beginning of the Fourth Epoch.

With no warring remnants left to act as successors to Hmurrath, the state of Eadraal absorbed all of the empire's former territory. Eadraal would become the largest nation in Serôs to survive into the Fourth Epoch, followed by the sea elven kingdom of Keryvyr.

Queen Selana brought a reformation to the mindsets and beliefs of the merfolk people, restoring age old traditions that had been lost under their prior subjugation by the elves of Aryselmalyr, such as clan loyalty. She also led them back into harmony with the other races of Serôs, asking them to forgive the shalarin for the devastation they caused to the Hmur Plateau during the war.

Combatants

Throughout the Tenth War, the Dukars remained uninvolved from its many civil wars. Up until, they intervened only to uphold the Laws of Battle as well as to protect bystanders and innocents. In 787 TS, for reasons unknown the Dukars withdrew completely from all involvement in Serôsian politics and by the end of the year none could be found anywhere.
Casualties
 Adoivaealumanth, a temple city in eastern Serôs built during the reign of the Aryselmalyr Empire, was left abandoned for centuries after some unknown forces were unleashed in  that spread a virulent disease, rendering the populace's gills raw and blistered.
 Baomal, the former capital of Hmurrath, was left in ruins.
 The city of Myth Nantar is considered fallen from the sahuagin invasion. Over the next 500 years folks would increasingly avoid swimming near it, referring to the ruins as cursed and that the haunting singing emanating from it were the ghosts of those slain by the sahuagin.
 An ixitxachitl baron's plans to conquer the Hmur Plateau were thwarted.
 The kraken Xisar's attempts to unite the morkoths and ixitxachitl of Serôs falls apart.
 Spirits of this war were believed to have joined the casualties of the Sixth and Eighth Serôs Wars in haunting the Haunted Plains.

## Tears of Aloevan

The Tears of Aloevan was the name of a magical pool of water within a glen of the northern Ardeep Forest called the Glen of Aloevan, and the hidden demiplane, a cloud of magic, accessible from this pond.

Description
The pond itself was clear.

The cloud of magic boasted a replica of the long-lost Court of Silver Fire, or the Court of Ardeep.The sources disagree on which court is replicated; though the latter source references the Court of Silver Fire, there are no known references to it anywhere else, and thus, this article is written under the assumption that's likely an error, and will be updated as more information becomes available.

Geography

The Tears of Aloevan lay within a glen called the Glen of Aloevan in the depths of the northern Ardeep Forest.It is considered a foregone conclusion that, as the map position is labeled "Glen of Aloevan" in the map key, and then named the Tears of Aloevan in the relevant chapter entry, they are in the same place, and thus, the glen described can only be the Glen of Aloevan.

Government
Embrae Aloevan ruled the demiplane, though her madness meant she could do very little without her seven attendants.

Defenses
The portal that led into the hidden realm would only open during solar eclipses. Besides Embrae herself, her seven attendants were powerful priests of Sehanine.

History
After the Laranla, Embrae Aloevan, passed away and was consumed by silver fire, her spirit was prevented from passing, remaining trapped within a nimbus of the silver fire. Her death also created a mystical pool. At a later date, seven clerics of Sehanine learned of this, and created a pathway to the otherworldly cloud of magic where her spirit was trapped. Once there, they took it upon themselves to recreate the Court of Silver Fire, or the Court of Ardeep, and ease the former queen's mental torment.

By the late 14 century DR, many outsiders had sought audience with the queen; none had returned.

Inhabitants
 Embrae Aloevan
